    61	## Contested points
    62	
    63	**CP1 — The role of SQLite.**
    64	- **opencode:** SQLite *is* the structured state store — "structured state tracking (ticket status, session logs, entity relationships)", written to `.ai/state.sqlite` inside the repo and queried at session start for `status != done`. Basis: **model knowledge** (C2), justified as "lightweight, queryable, no external service needed, atomic writes".
    65	- **claude and codex:** SQLite is at most a **derived, gitignored, rebuildable cache** and never authoritative. claude places it at `.cache/` "rebuilt from the tree"; codex places it at `.cache/project-memory/index.sqlite` gitignored and requires that "its absence must not prevent reading authoritative memory", refusing a committed SQLite as sole memory because "it adds a required access mechanism where inspectable files already meet the proposed need". Basis: codex `read: https://sqlite.org/fts5.html` plus its **model-knowledge** recommendation; claude's T1/T2/T4 design tests.
    66	- **unresolved.** Nothing in the three files measures a committed binary state store against markdown state files. What would settle it: on one real project, track ticket/session state both ways and compare merge-conflict rate on the binary, the cost of querying state without the DB (does a reader still recover?), and the human reviewability of a `state.sqlite` diff.
    67	
    68	**CP2 — Contradiction resolution: newest-wins vs disputed-and-escalate.**
    69	- **opencode:** "On read, agent should check for `## Superseded` / `## Stale` markers and **prefer the newest non-stale entry**." Basis: **model knowledge** (C7, staleness section).
    70	- **claude and codex:** newest does not win. claude: "**Why not newest wins.** Graphiti's 'new wins' is write-time and LLM-driven"; both entries become `contested`, the next agent adjudicates against code and tests, a human decides if code cannot. Basis: **read: FINDINGS-s4 F15, F16** — a deterministic latest-wins pipeline scored far above the stores on MemoryAgentBench single-hop (78.0%/94.8% vs Zep 7.0%, Mem0 18.0%) but only tied LLM judgment on LongMemEval knowledge-update (57.8% vs 64.4%, n=45); claude gives both rows and refuses to pick. codex: "Recent timestamps, majority votes and clean Git merges are insufficient to select a winner." Basis: **model knowledge** (C22).
    71	- **unresolved.** No engine ran read-time newest-wins against disputed-and-escalate on real coding tasks. What would settle it: replay both rules over a recorded set of contradicting entries and count how often newest-wins selects the entry that a later code change or a maintainer confirms.
    72	
    73	**CP3 — Where the memory tree lives.**
    74	- **opencode:** `.ai/`, **isolated from human docs** — "keeps AI memory isolated from human docs". Basis: **model knowledge**.
    75	- **codex:** under the project's **existing `docs/`** — `docs/memory/`, `docs/decisions/`, `docs/work/` — and "reuse existing project documentation rather than copying it into memory"; memory links to already-authoritative requirements and architecture. Basis: **model knowledge** (C13).
    76	- **claude:** a dedicated committed **`memory/` tree** at the repo root (`INDEX.md`, `entries/`, `state/`, `journal/`). Basis: partly **read: REPORT.md s1-F7/F8** (Letta's layout: root files load every turn, directories with their own `MEMORY.md` stay out of context), partly design.
    77	- **unresolved.** This is a convention question, and the three files contain no evidence about a real project's docs. What would settle it: an inventory of one project's existing docs — if `docs/` already holds authoritative requirements and architecture, codex's linking placement wins; if writing agent memory there would pollute human docs, opencode's isolation wins; claude's separate committed tree is a third option.
    78	
    79	**CP4 — One shared per-day session file vs per-writer files.**
    80	- **opencode:** `sessions/YYYY-MM-DD.md`, append-only, one file per day, all agents appending. Basis: **model knowledge** (layout and write table).
    81	- **claude and codex:** one writer per file. claude: "Different file per session and per claim… No two agents edit one file", and per-session files "avoid git merge conflicts that a single shared append file causes" (**model knowledge**, C35). codex refuses "Parallel agents appending to one shared journal" — "overlapping writes and leaves integration implicit" — and notes that "Git merges do not establish semantic agreement". Basis: **read: https://git-scm.com/book/en/v2/Git-Branching-Basic-Branching-and-Merging**.
    82	- **unresolved.** No engine measured conflict rates. What would settle it: run several agents concurrently on one repo with a shared append file and with per-writer files, and count merge conflicts, lost appends and time spent integrating.
    83	
    84	**CP5 — Whether an agent's private store may persist between sessions.**
    85	- **codex:** yes — "allow for personal continuity; refuse as a project dependency"; private memory "may suggest where to look", and useful knowledge is promoted into the repo. Basis: **read: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory#storage-location** plus **model knowledge**.
    86	- **opencode:** no — the private store "should be ephemeral — no persistent memory between sessions"; cross-session state "should be explicit in project memory, not hidden in agent internals". Basis: **model knowledge** (C6).
    87	- **claude** is silent on whether it should persist: it records that each agent keeps a private store with no shared schema (C32, **model knowledge**; C31, **read:** the local `MEMORY.md` index), and rules only that a fact existing only there "does not count as memory".
    88	- **unresolved.** No evidence on whether private persistence helps continuity or hides state other agents need. What would settle it: an A/B where one agent keeps private notes and another does not, measuring whether the notes change actions and whether they duplicate what is in the repo.
    89	
    90	**CP6 — What is read automatically at session start.**
    91	- **opencode:** `context.md`, active tickets from `state.sqlite`, **and "Recent `sessions/` (last 3)"** for continuity. Basis: **model knowledge**.
    92	- **claude and codex:** instructions, the index, and the assigned task's state only. claude: `AGENTS.md`, `INDEX.md`, the `state/claims/` listing, the newest handoff — and "**Never load.** `journal/`". codex: instructions, `docs/memory/INDEX.md`, the task's `state.md`; "Do not bulk-load transcripts, completed-task journals, every decision or an entire search index at startup." Basis: codex **read: OpenAI guidance on skills and prompts** plus **model knowledge**; claude's read rules are design.
    93	- **unresolved.** No engine measured whether three prior session summaries improve the first action more than they cost in tokens and distraction. What would settle it: a with/without comparison on recorded tasks scoring first-action correctness and start-up tokens.
    94	
    95	**CP7 — Whether a memory write is obligatory every session.**
    96	- **opencode:** yes — "Session end | agent | Write summary to `sessions/YYYY-MM-DD.md` | Yes". Basis: **model knowledge**.
    97	- **codex:** no — "Do not require a memory write after every session. Write when there is something another session needs to know", and it refuses "a complete transcript archive as routine memory". Basis: **model knowledge** (C14).
    98	- **claude** sits between: writes are at task end and "Not every turn", but it does permit per-session `journal/` and `handoff/` appends; the raw-capture read (C9) supports keeping raw material cheap.
    99	- **unresolved.** This is a work-practice question the three files cannot settle. What would settle it: measure, over many sessions, how often a mandatory session summary is later read or cited — if it is rarely read, codex's rule wins; if it prevents repeated mistakes, opencode's does.
   100	
   101	**A separate, narrow note (not a claim conflict).** claude's C25 concludes from its read material — LoCoMo's key has 6.4% score-corrupting errors, and swapping only the grading prompt moved one system from 0.9013 to 0.4803 — that "published accuracy numbers cannot choose a project-memory design". codex reaches a related caution from a different source (C12, Liu et al., position-sensitive retrieval) but bounds it: "That establishes a historical failure mode, not a performance result for today's coding agents." The two do not disagree; codex's is the narrower claim.
   102	
   103	## Substrates
   104	
   105	| substrate | merged answer | reason | engines |
   106	|---|---|---|---|
   107	| Markdown files | **USE — source of truth** | Human-readable, diff-reviewable, rebuildable, no service. Every engine's primary substrate (M1, M22). The ranking itself is model knowledge; only the git-backed property is read (claude, REPORT.md s1-F7/F8). | claude, codex, opencode |
   108	| SQLite | **CONDITIONAL — derived, gitignored, rebuildable only; never authoritative** (majority). opencode dissents (CP1) | Codex: "allow as an optional derived index; refuse as the default authoritative memory"; absence must not prevent reading memory. claude: `.cache/`, rebuilt from the tree. opencode would use it as the structured state store. | claude, codex (opencode contested) |
   109	| Vector index | **REFUSE as the store; CONDITIONAL as a rebuildable derived index, only if the project's own queries justify it** | Similarity does not establish that a statement is current, accepted or true (codex, model knowledge); the one directly comparable read found relevance recall and token cost "largely equivalent" to a KG (claude, read REPORT.md s3-F34). | claude, codex, opencode |
   110	| Graph store | **REFUSE as the store; CONDITIONAL as a derived code map generated per commit** | No demonstrated generic need for a relationship model; supersession is available as frontmatter links; graph evidence is split and a live graph has a resident-context cost and can drift silently (claude, read REPORT.md s3 group). | claude, codex, opencode |
   111	| Context window | **USE for the current task only; REFUSE as durable memory** | Fresh context at session start; recovery must be possible from project artifacts without the previous conversation (codex, read Claude Code memory docs). | claude, codex, opencode |
   112	| Each agent's private session store | **REFUSE as a project dependency; allow private notes but promote anything others need into the repo** (persistence itself is CP5) | It is machine-local and invisible to other agents; a fact that exists only there does not count as project memory (codex read storage location; claude C31/C32; opencode C6). | claude, codex, opencode |
   113	
   114	## Layout
   115	
   116	One committed memory tree. The placement of that tree is CP3 (claude `memory/`; codex under `docs/`; opencode
   117	`.ai/`). The merged default shown here uses claude's typed-entry shape and codex's per-task shape, with the
   118	generated index both propose.
   119	
   120	```
   121	AGENTS.md                  canonical standing instructions: commands, constraints, memory read/write protocol, links.
   122	CLAUDE.md                  thin adapter / symlink importing AGENTS.md — no independently maintained policy copy.
   123	memory/
   124	  INDEX.md                 GENERATED, one line per active entry: id · kind · title · covers · status flag.
   125	                           Never hand-edited. The only whole-file memory load at session start.
   126	  entries/
   127	    D-0007-<slug>.md       decision + why: context, choice, consequences, acceptance reference, replacement links.
   128	    F-0012-<slug>.md       sourced observation / verified pitfall not derivable from code, tests or git history.
   129	    L-0003-<slug>.md       lesson from a failure or a human correction.
   130	    R-0002-<slug>.md       runbook; prefer a script, the entry only points at it.
   131	  work/<task-id>/
   132	    state.md               current handoff: objective, owner, branch/base revision, done, outstanding, checks, next action.
   133	    events/<event-id>.md   writer-specific historical record: a discovery, a failed approach, a handoff.
   134	    evidence/              sanitized evidence not recoverable from code, commits, tests or linked artifacts.
   135	  journal/<date>-<agent>-<session>.md   append-only raw notes; never auto-loaded.
   136	  .cache/ (gitignored)     optional derived SQLite/FTS and a repo map, rebuilt from the tree at the current commit.
   137	docs/ ...                  the project's existing requirements, architecture and runbooks; memory links here, does not copy.
   138	```
   139	
   140	Entry metadata (union of claude's frontmatter and codex's block; use applicable fields): `id`, `kind`
   141	(decision | fact | lesson | runbook | observation | hypothesis), `status`
   142	(proposed | active | contested | stale | disputed | superseded | retired), `author`, `recorded_at`,
   143	`observed_at` / `verified_at` (plus the git revision verified against), `covers` (paths or symbols),
   144	`source` / `evidence`, `depends_on`, `supersedes`, `superseded_by`. Body: the claim in one or two sentences,
   145	then **Why:**, then **How to apply:**.
   146	
   147	## Write rules
   148	
   149	- **Triggers (write):** a decision whose reason is not visible in code; a non-obvious constraint or verified
   150	  pitfall that cost more than one attempt; a human correction; a checkpoint of unfinished work before a
   151	  handoff, interruption or context reduction, recording exact remaining work and actual verification results,
   152	  including failures (claude, codex; opencode adds a decision and a session summary — CP7 for the summary).
   153	- **Do not write:** anything derivable from code, tests or git history; credentials or sensitive data;
   154	  speculative explanations unlabelled (label them `hypothesis`); bulk transcripts; another agent's private
   155	  state. Do not turn "implemented" into "verified".
   156	- **When:** at discovery or correction, or at task end, in the same commit or PR as the change that motivated
   157	  the entry. Not every turn; not as a standing per-session obligation (CP7).
   158	- **Who (agents):** append `journal/` and a handoff; create entries as `proposed` with `evidence:` mandatory;
   159	  update the authoritative document that a change invalidates, in the same review; flag `stale` or `contested`
   160	  with evidence; claim a task by creating a claim file.
   161	- **Who (humans):** accept `proposed` → `active` (review of the diff is the merge gate); accept significant
   162	  decisions and policy; resolve `contested` when code cannot; retire entries; set the index cap and the age
   163	  threshold. (claude, codex; opencode's write table has agents authoring decisions with no acceptance step —
   164	  recorded as a gap, not as a merged rule.)
   165	- **Append-only:** `journal/`, `work/<task-id>/events/`, evidence, git history, and an accepted decision's body
   166	  (after acceptance only `status`, `verified_at` and `superseded_by` may change; to change the claim, write a
   167	  new entry). Sensitive material is redacted despite the historical policy.
   168	- **Mutable, but by diff:** `INDEX.md` (generated), current summaries, and fact/lesson bodies.
   169	- **Concurrency:** different file per session and per claim, one writer per file; no two agents edit one file
   170	  (CP4 — opencode's shared per-day file is the dissent).
   171	- **Never** store project knowledge only in an agent's private memory and call it memory.
   172	
   173	## Read rules
   174	
   175	- **At session start (always):** `AGENTS.md`; `memory/INDEX.md`; the `work/` listing and the assigned task's
   176	  `state.md`. Enforce this with a SessionStart hook or an explicit first instruction in `AGENTS.md`, because
   177	  agents skip optional reads. Establish the actual checkout and working-tree state before trusting a handoff.
   178	  (CP6 governs whether the last few session files are also read.)
   179	- **On demand:** an entry body when its `covers` match a path about to be edited (a PreToolUse hook or an
   180	  `AGENTS.md` instruction); filename and text search, or FTS, over `memory/entries/`; `git log -S`, `git blame`
   181	  and PR comments for line-level "why"; a repo map or symbol search generated fresh for the current commit;
   182	  for "why" questions read the decision entry rather than relying on similarity retrieval.
   183	- **Open the source when it matters:** re-read the underlying file, test or commit when a retrieved statement
   184	  will determine an action, and re-read affected memory after an integration or branch change.
   185	- **Never load:** `journal/`; entries marked `superseded` or `retired`, except by following a link from a
   186	  loaded entry; the whole `memory/` tree; a full graph or code-map dump; bulk issue/PR bodies; another agent's
   187	  private transcripts; credentials; retrieved text treated as authority that can override standing instructions.
   188	- **Budget:** the index must stay under a cap and the check script fails the build if it does not. The merged
   189	  answer gives **no number** — claude left the cap, the age threshold and the write-trigger set to the owner
   190	  ("never fill a gap with my own value"). Over the cap, consolidate off the critical path.
   191	
   192	## Staleness and contradiction
   193	
   194	- **Dating:** every entry has an ISO `recorded_at`, an `observed_at`/`verified_at`, and the git revision it was
   195	  verified against. A date alone says nothing about truth (claude, codex).
   196	- **Staleness is computed, not guessed:** a deterministic script (claude's `memory_check.py`; no model call)
   197	  diffs `covers` between `verified_at` and HEAD and shows a `⚠ drifted` flag in `INDEX.md`, which the reader sees
   198	  before relying on the entry. The script never rewrites the entry. Re-verifying updates `verified_at`;
   199	  confirming the entry false sets `status: stale` and starts supersession. `depends_on` links flag downstream
   200	  entries rather than recompute them. (M15, M16; opencode's date-stamped `## Stale as of …` marker is
   201	  compatible but not change-triggered.)
   202	- **Supersession:** never delete. The new entry carries `supersedes: [id]`; the old one gets `status: superseded`
   203	  and `superseded_by`. No LLM and no database (claude, read: Graphiti/Mem0 use the same invalidate-don't-remove
   204	  shape). This covers the question types where the KG beat vector top-k.
   205	- **Contradiction:** if one entry supersedes the other, that is a valid chain. Otherwise both become `contested`
   206	  and the index shows both. The next agent to touch that area adjudicates against code and tests and writes a
   207	  superseding entry, or flags a retire with evidence; if code cannot decide, a human decides. Record the
   208	  resolution and link both earlier assertions. While unresolved, mark the subject disputed and stop only the
   209	  action that depends on choosing a side; independent work continues. **Never average conflicting measurements
   210	  or pick a winner silently** — this is where claude and codex part from opencode's newest-wins rule (CP2).
   211	- **No time-decay deletion:** the decay evidence conflicts in the read material (claude: Selective Forgetting
   212	  removes 9.8% of nodes with token F1 unchanged — C15 — versus EngramRAG naming temporal decay evicting core
   213	  invariants a defect — C16; the source report leaves the two unresolved). Age may only add a flag. The
   214	  threshold is the owner's to set.
   215	- **Consolidation:** periodic, off the critical path, as a proposed PR — merge duplicates, retire dead entries,
   216	  shrink the index — never a silent rewrite.
   217	
   218	## What NOT to build
   219	
   220	Three refusal tests were offered, and they are **given as three, not averaged**: **T-A (codex, model
   221	knowledge, unexecuted):** add a store only for a named, demonstrated failure of the simpler system, with source
   222	traceability, a maintenance owner and an evaluated benefit. **T-B (claude):** git-rebuildable from a fresh clone
   223	with no model call; diff-reviewable before it takes effect; not a second truth needing sync against code and
   224	git; beats the flat-markdown baseline at a matched token budget on this project's own questions; one maker per
   225	field. **T-C (opencode, model knowledge):** does it solve a pain the agent actually feels; does it avoid
   226	external infrastructure; can humans read and verify it. A store is refused if it fails any applicable test.
   227	
   228	| Refused | Refused by | Test it fails, and why |
   229	|---|---|---|
   230	| Vector database as project truth | claude, codex, opencode | T-B (not human-readable, becomes a second truth), T-A (no demonstrated need), T-C (Q3). A derived FTS/vector cache is allowed, never authoritative. |
   231	| Graph database as the store (Graphiti/Zep-style, Neo4j) | claude, codex, opencode | T-A (no demonstrated generic relationship need), T-B (not diff-reviewable; supersession is available as frontmatter links), T-C. claude adds that Mem0 removed graph support from its open-source SDK in v2.0.0 (its own label: reported, third-party). |
   232	| Committed SQLite as the sole or authoritative memory | claude, codex | T-A/T-B — "it adds a required access mechanism where inspectable files already meet the proposed need"; absence must not prevent reading memory. **opencode dissents: CP1.** |
   233	| LLM-chosen ADD/UPDATE/DELETE, or auto-extraction of facts from every turn | claude | T-B (destructive, not diff-reviewable); hallucinations "accumulate at memory extraction and updating, then propagate into answers" (read: REPORT.md s5-F5); raw capture is cheaper and non-inferior at a tight budget (read: FINDINGS-s4 F17/F18). |
   234	| Always-live code graph / auto-synced index | claude | T-B — the project's own benchmark measured 4.3% of distinct edges wrong on a clean rebuild, silently, and a CLI over the same index served stale answers (read: REPORT.md s3-F25/F27); plus the resident-context cost. Generate per commit, as a cache. |
   235	| A giant, automatically growing `MEMORY.md` or whole-tree load | claude, codex, opencode | T-C (readable is not enough — compulsory reading), T-B (unrelated subjects combined); context cost. The index is the only load-at-start item. |
   236	| Parallel agents appending to one shared journal | claude, codex | T-B — overlapping writes and implicit integration; a merge does not establish semantic agreement. **opencode's per-day `sessions/` file is this pattern: CP4.** |
   237	| A complete transcript archive as routine memory | claude, codex, opencode | T-A/T-C — preserves volume without selecting what future work needs; opencode's own read rule refuses full history. |
   238	| Time-based auto-expiry or decay deletion | claude | T-B — it is not reversible and the decay evidence conflicts (C15 vs C16); age may only flag. |
   239	| Agent-private store as project memory | claude, codex, opencode | T-B (invisible to human review), T-C (Q3), and it breaks one-maker-per-field. |
   240	| Cloud KV / external sync as memory | opencode | T-C (Q2 external infrastructure and offline failure mode). |
   241	| A custom memory service before a retrieval failure exists | codex | T-A — benefit and maintenance obligations not established. |
   242	| Ownership files or CODEOWNERS as memory | claude | T-A/T-B — it is review routing, not retrievable memory; the read material stores no such memory role. |
   243	
   244	Each refuser's own caveat is carried forward: claude's tests are its design, not executed; codex "authored this
   245	test and have not executed it", so its refusals mean "not justified as a generic default", not "proved
   246	ineffective"; opencode's three questions are model knowledge. SQLite indexing, semantic retrieval and graph
   247	queries all remain possible additions **once their particular benefit is demonstrated**.
   248	
   249	## Strength of this recommendation
   250	
   251	- **How many merged claims rest on a source an engine actually read.** Of the 24 rows: **19 rest on a source at
   252	  least one engine read**, and **5 rest on `model knowledge` at core** — M1 (the markdown-over-others ranking,
   253	  where the adjacent read supports only git-backed files), M6 (SQLite's derived-only role, where the read
   254	  establishes only that FTS5 exists), M8 (private-store persistence), M22 (the human-readability refusal), and
   255	  M24 (write cadence). None of the model-knowledge labels was upgraded.
   256	- **But the read basis is mostly second-hand.** 13 of the 19 read-backed rows rest on claude's reading of the
   257	  project's own research report (`docs/research/20261011-agent-memory-graph-r2/REPORT.md` and
   258	  `FINDINGS-s4-retrieval-consolidation.md`), which claude itself says restates `INDEX.md` and "says no number in
   259	  it was independently re-run". Those rows are "an engine read a real file"; they are not primary evidence, and
   260	  where claude gives two conflicting rows (C10/C11, C13/C14, C15/C16, C17/C18, C26/C27) **both are kept here and
   261	  neither is chosen**.
   262	- **Which engines read real material at all.** **claude** read local material: `~/CLAUDE.md`, the project
   263	  `CLAUDE.md`, the Claude Code `MEMORY.md` index, the project's `REPORT.md` and one findings file — and said
   264	  once that it did not use web search. **codex** read primary sources: git-scm.com, sqlite.org/fts5,
   265	  developers.openai.com (retrieval guide and skills/prompts post), code.claude.com/docs/en/memory,
   266	  opencode.ai/docs/rules, learn.chatgpt.com AGENTS.md, arxiv.org/abs/2307.03172, cognitect.com ADR essay, plus
   267	  local files (`CLAUDE.md`, the requirement-check skill). **opencode read nothing**: all 7 of its claims are
   268	  labelled `model knowledge`, it has no `read:` row, and it did not state (as the brief required) that it had no
   269	  web access. It also states a number it did not read — "Does this fit in 64k tokens?" — contrary to the brief's
   270	  evidence standard.
   271	- **opencode is thin and partly off-brief, and is used accordingly.** 7 claims against codex's 26 and claude's
   272	  36; zero sources; it never addresses the cross-agent instruction file at all, although the brief names
   273	  several agents and asks for a generic project; it reports "Confidence: High" from model knowledge alone. Its
   274	  contribution here is therefore a third opinion on **shape** (markdown primary, append-only supersession,
   275	  human-readable, refuse stores without a felt need) plus the two genuine disagreements CP1 and CP2 — not
   276	  evidence.
   277	- **Shared blind spots.** No engine produced an operational definition of "work well" (codex flags that it
   278	  "fails weak-words and can-it-fail checks" and leaves it open); no engine ran memory-with against
   279	  memory-without on a real project; the index size cap, the age threshold and the write-trigger set are the
   280	  owner's to set and no engine would invent them; and agreement between claude and codex is partly agreement
   281	  between one secondary report and one set of vendor docs reasoning, not two independent measurements.
   282	
   283	## Digest
   284	
   285	- **Answer.** Git-versioned markdown with typed, dated, evidence-bearing entries and explicit supersession is
   286	  the source of truth; a generated index plus the task state file are the only start-up reads; SQLite/FTS,
   287	  vector and code-graph stores are gitignored derived caches; no store, DB, context window or private agent
   288	  memory is authoritative.
   289	- **Confidence.** Medium in the shape (19 of 24 merged claims rest on a source an engine read); low in every
   290	  number, because the strongest numbers come from the project's own secondary report.
   291	- **Cautions.** Seven points are contested: SQLite's role, newest-wins, where memory lives, the shared session
   292	  file, private-store persistence, the session-start read set, and write cadence. opencode read nothing and
   293	  never answered the cross-agent instruction question.
   294	- **Gaps.** "Work well" was never made operational; no with/without measurement; the index cap, age threshold
   295	  and write triggers are left to the owner.
   296	- **Decision it triggers.** Adopt the markdown-and-index baseline on one project, set the index cap, and run a
   297	  with-memory against without-memory comparison on about a dozen recorded past tasks before any derived or
   298	  additional store is adopted.

    24	## By category
    25	
    26	`file hit@5` counts a gold `path::name` as hit when the route returns its file. `symbol hit@5` needs the symbol itself: an entry naming it, the top-level def/class holding it or a method of it, a module-code chunk for a module-level name, and for `rg` a printed match line inside the symbol's lines. A gold given as a plain file scores the same on both. **The best route is chosen on symbol hit@5.**
    27	
    28	`chars` is the mean number of characters of output read up to and including the first line where the route names a gold file as its answer (`# <path> (`, or a line starting `<path>:`), over the answers that named one; `n` is how many did. A miss is left out, never scored 0. What each route prints differs -- rg: a `# path (N matching lines)` header per file plus at most 40 match lines of up to 300 characters; fts: one 24-token snippet per path; vec: the first 300 characters per path; hybrid: the vec or fts block per path; graph: one id per line -- so `chars` measures the print format as much as the store.

-   0.00–0.01 s on this repo (`G-F12`; code-graph node §6). Git is the record itself, so it cannot drift.

+categories first"*). Each requirement names its source: **(v)** is the vision, **(d)** is this document's text, and

-- **R1.1** Deleting the cache and rebuilding from the files restores every answer. *Example:* `rm -rf
-  project-memory/.cache/` → run the documented rebuild → the same query returns the same entries. (M6)
-- **R1.2** Memory is fully readable with no cache present. *Example:* with `.cache/` absent, session start and an
-  on-demand `grep` over `entries/` both work. (M6)

-  *Example:* the rebuild command has no "update" mode. (G-F5 drift)

-  commit or a content hash of its sources, and the query tool compares before answering. A newest-mtime check is not
-  enough: a deletion leaves no newer file (R3.1). (S-F9)

-### R3 — Stale never answers

-  rebuilds if they differ, building into a temporary file and renaming over the old one — so a deleted entry cannot

-  treated as absent. *Example:* point the tool at a typo'd path → it warns and falls back to grep, it does not
-  create an empty database. (G-F5, failure mode 5)

+  or with no decision by EJ to build it.
+
+**B2 — Derived from the files** (v: never the source of truth; was R1)
+The cache is computed from the memory's markdown and never holds knowledge of its own (M4, M6).
+
+- **B2.1** Deleting the cache and rebuilding from the files restores every answer, provided the vector index is
+  rebuilt with the same embedding model (a changed model makes every vector useless, V-F24). *Example:* `rm -rf
+  project-memory/.cache/` → run the documented rebuild → the same query returns the same entries. (M6) (was R1.1)
+- **B2.2** The cache directory is ignored by git and a rebuild leaves `git status --short` clean. (M6) (was R1.3)
+- **Fails if** the same query returns different entries after a delete and a rebuild with the same embedding model,
+  or a rebuild leaves `git status --short` dirty.
+
+**B3 — Full rebuild, never patched** (v: never patched; was R2.1)
+- **B3.1** The documented rebuild regenerates the whole index from the files; no incremental update path exists.
+  *Example:* the rebuild command has no "update" mode. (G-F5 drift)
+- **Fails if** the rebuild has an update or incremental mode.
+
+**B4 — Only active entries, with their status** (d: Rules for all three caches, my reading; was R6)
+- **B4.1** The index holds the same set of entries as `INDEX.md`, each row carrying `status` and `covers`.
+  *Example:* a superseded entry never appears in a search result as if current. (V-F22, my reading)
+- **Fails if** the index holds an entry `INDEX.md` does not list as active, lacks one that `INDEX.md` does list, or
+  has a row without `status` or `covers`.
+
+**B5 — No model call to rebuild, except embedding** (v: How we know it works; was R8.1)
+- **B5.1** The documented rebuild completes without a model call, except the embedding step of B1.2.
+  *Example:* rebuild with no model credentials available; the full-text path still works. (M4, M6)
+- **Fails if** the full-text rebuild calls a model, or the vector rebuild calls any model but its embedding model.
+

 **The problem it solves.** The memory is markdown in git, and today `INDEX.md` plus grep finds what an agent needs —
-at 10,000 entries a grep takes 0.038–0.06 s (`S-F13`). It stops being enough as the memory grows, for two reasons:

+**K1 — It records what it was built from** (d: Rules for all three caches; was R2.2)
+- **K1.1** The index records a content hash of the entry files it was built from, so a stale one is detectable,
+  including edits not yet committed (*proposed*: a commit alone misses those). A newest-mtime check is not enough:
+  a deletion leaves no newer file (K2.1). (S-F9)
+- **K1.2** The vector index also records the id of the embedding model it was built with, because a changed model
+  makes every vector useless (V-F24; the vector 5W1H stores it beside each vector).
+- **Fails if** the index carries no content hash of its sources, or the vector index carries no embedding-model id.
+
+**K2 — Stale never answers** (d: Rules for all three caches; was R3.1)
+- **K2.1** Before answering, the tool compares the recorded content hash with the entry files, and for the vector
+  index the recorded model id with the current one (*proposed*). If either differs it rebuilds, building into a
+  temporary file and renaming over the old one — so a deleted entry cannot stay searchable. (S-F9)
+- **Fails if** after an entry is added, changed or deleted, or the embedding model changes, a query answers from the
+  old index.
+
+**K3 — Dropped when it stops paying** (d; vision: *"cheap … and not before"*; was R8.2; EJ, 2026-10-12: *"yes please
+write"*)
+- **K3.1** A built cache keeps passing its own trigger: its B1 comparison is rerun on the recorded questions (Rd4),
+  and a query's cost includes any rebuild it triggered (K2) (*proposed*).
+- **K3.2** The comparison is rerun when an agent records a miss, and when EJ asks; the agent that runs it writes the
+  result to `runs/<run-id>/` (*proposed*, the same as B1.2).
+- **K3.3** If the cache no longer passes, the agent tells EJ, who decides whether to drop it. Dropping is deleting it;
+  memory is unaffected (B2, Rd3) (*proposed*, mirroring B1.0).
+- **Fails if** a cache stays in `project-memory/.cache/` after a rerun it failed, with no decision by EJ to keep it.
+

+  on-demand `grep` over `entries/` both work. (M6) (was R1.2)

+  grep, not the cache, so the agent knows its results are unranked. (S-F9) (was R4.1; *proposed*: "says so in every

    40	## The answer first
    41	
    42	None of the three caches is needed today **for memory entries**. `project-memory/` does not exist yet, so nothing has
    43	been measured on it. My reading of the research is that at the sizes measured elsewhere, `INDEX.md`, grep and git
    44	answer the questions each cache would answer. Each cache below has a
    45	**trigger**: a condition that can be observed and that makes it worth building. None of them is built before its
    46	trigger fires.
    47	
    48	The caches are for **memory entries** only: that is the vision EJ confirmed on 2026-10-12. The measured section below
    49	on code and docs is kept as evidence; it is outside the vision, and its proposals to widen the vision were not adopted.
    50	
    51	This follows the design's own rule: `.cache/` is optional, gitignored and rebuilt from the files, and its absence
    52	must not stop anyone reading memory (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md`; recommendation M4, M6). It also follows the repo's own
    53	history of building graphs nobody queried (`docs/KNOWLEDGE_GRAPH_DESIGN.md` §1, quoted in `G-F21`).
    54	
    55	## When to use which store
    56	
    57	The two tables below cover two different corpora and are kept apart. **Part 1 is for memory entries**
    58	(`project-memory/`), reasoned from the research and the run record. **Part 2 is for code and docs**, measured on this
    59	repo. In both parts a cache is derived and optional and is never the source of truth; once its trigger fires it is a
    60	cheaper way to reach the same entry.
    61	
    62	### Part 1 — memory entries (corpus: `project-memory/`)
    63	
    64	At query time the memory is answered by the files — `INDEX.md`, grep, git, or reading the entry — and the files are
    65	the source of truth, not the cache. The table below merges three engines asked independently, one store
    66	each: `claude` on the code graph, `codex` on the vector index, `opencode` on SQLite FTS5. The full 21-row table and
    67	the 30-row cost table, with every disagreement kept, are in `runs/20261012-caches-enhance/DECISION-RULES.md` — the
    68	run record. **This file is the live copy.**
    69	
    70	**The rule.** **SQLite FTS5** is for a free-text question whose words are likely in the entry text, once `INDEX.md`
    71	can no longer list every active entry under its size limit and ranked results with snippets save opened files
    72	(`S-F1`, `S-F4`). **The vector index** is for the same discovery when the question's wording differs from the
    73	entry's, once the documented miss trigger has fired (`V-F11`, `V-F13`, `M4`). **The code graph** is only for a
    74	multi-hop structural question about Python code, after its trigger, and only as a lower bound (`G-F8`, `G-F17`,
    75	`G-F18`, `M5`). Everything else is the files.
    76	
    77	| the question | store | if not, this | evidence |
    78	|---|---|---|---|
    79	| What do we already know about X? (free text, words likely in the entry) | **FTS5** — conditional on the size trigger | `INDEX.md`, grep, then read | S-F1, S-F4 |
    80	| Which prior entry matches when my words differ from its? (find by meaning) | **vector** — conditional on the miss trigger | `INDEX.md`, grep, then read | V-F11, V-F13, M4 |
    81	| A question about the code itself: what changing F reaches, which tests reach F | see **Part 2** (code and docs) | — | Part 2 |
    82	| An exact identifier, an error string, a known `file:line` | none | literal grep or filename lookup, then read | V-F7, V-F21, s3-F29 |
    83	| Every active entry covering path X — completeness | none — a ranked top-k cannot prove completeness | exhaustive scan of `INDEX.md` and entry headers | V-F1, S-F13, G-F14 |
    84	| Proving absence ("have we never recorded…") | none — no hit is not proof | exhaustive search, then read | V-F20 |
    85	| A structured header question (status, `supersedes`, `depends_on`) | none | `INDEX.md` scan or a script over frontmatter; follow links by grep | V-F22, M13 |
    86	| Why did we choose this, or why is this line like this? | none | the entry's **Why:** and its evidence; `git blame`, `git log -S` | M19, SOURCE_OF_TRUTH |
    87	| Is this decision still true of the code? | none | read the entry and the code before acting on it; a mismatch is reported to EJ | SOURCE_OF_TRUTH Rd5 |
    88	| What should I load when resuming? | none | `INDEX.md` and the task's `state.md` | M17, M18, M19 |
    89	| A map of the whole repo | none | `PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md`, `INDEX.md` | M18, G-F18 |
    90	| A `covers:` path that is not Python | none | git and grep | G-F18 |
    91	| Is the graph I am about to query current? | none — do not check, rebuild whole | rebuild, or use git | G-F5, G-F18 |
    92	
    93	**Costs, and where there are none.** Measured on other corpora or on this machine, never on a real
    94	`project-memory/`: grep over 10,000 synthetic entries, 0.038 s for one word and 0.060 s for a structured chain
    95	(`S-F13`); grep plus opening every hit, about 226K tokens per question (`S-F4`, `S-F13` — opencode's figure, and D3
    96	records that the other two report no such measurement; `S-F4` measures other corpora, not memory entries); an
    97	`INDEX.md` scan, under 0.01 s and minimal tokens
    98	(`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md`); reading one entry, roughly 270 tokens by the 4-characters-per-token assumption (`S-F5`, not
    99	measured); a git last-change lookup at the tool's timing resolution (`G-F12`). **`GAP` for everything that matters
   100	most**: no FTS5-vs-grep measurement on short entries (`S-F5`), no vector measurement on a corpus shaped like
   101	`entries/` (`V-F11`), no graph-vs-grep head-to-head on this repo's own questions (`G-F10`, `M21`), and no wrapper
   102	exists to time a filtered graph query (`G-F18`). The graph-vs-grep head-to-head is now measured on this repo's own
   103	code and docs (BM-1–BM-9, below); the vector-on-`entries/` gap and a real `project-memory/` corpus are not. See
   104	**Measured here: development and testing** below.
   105	
   106	**Still unresolved.** Six disagreements the three engines did not settle, kept as positions and not averaged (D1–D6
   107	in the run record): whether FTS5 or the vector index comes first, or whether memory needs a store at all; whether the
   108	FTS/vector benefit has been measured at all; the true token cost of grep-plus-open; the cost of `INDEX.md`; whether
   109	`INDEX.md` carries `covers:` and status; and what counts as a *fired* trigger. **For code and docs, the measurements
   110	settle three of them** (my reading): D1, full-text before vector (BM-4, BM-5); D2, the full-text benefit is measured
   111	on docs (BM-4, BM-6); D3, grep printed about 4,470 characters to the right file on decision questions, not ~226K
   112	tokens (BM-4). For memory entries all six stay open.
   113	
   114	**Why no SQLite is visible in this repo.** Three reasons, and none is a rejection. (Since 2026-10-12 three SQLite
   115	caches do exist, in the measurement run's workspace: `runs/20261012-caches-routing/bench/.cache/` holds `fts.sqlite`,
   116	`vec.sqlite` and `graph.sqlite`, 9.5, 10.2 and 4.9 MB (BM-9). They are gitignored and rebuilt there, not part of the
   117	product.) The patterns are gitignored and
   118	the indexes are rebuilt on demand. The SQLite this repo does have, `ancient_games/index.py`,
   119	indexes the **referee's journal**, not memory: it is the working precedent for this exact pattern (7 tables, derived,
   120	~8 ms per journal) but it answers a different question. And the memory cache is gated behind a trigger that has not
   121	fired, because `project-memory/` does not exist yet. The same is true of the code graph: `code_graph/code_graph.py`
   122	already exists, deterministic and full-rebuild, and is the option this document names if that trigger fires.
   123	
   124	A fourth reason belongs to the first draft. Its research asked only whether each cache is needed to search memory
   125	*entries*, so it measured nothing on the questions asked during development and testing. The section **Measured here:
   126	development and testing** measures those.
   127	
   128	### Part 2 — code and docs (corpus: this repo's code and docs)
   129	
   130	**Outside the vision.** The confirmed vision covers memory entries only (EJ, 2026-10-12). This table is measured on
   131	this repo's code and docs and is kept as evidence and as guidance for agents; it is not part of the caches design.
   132	Its evidence is **Measured here: development and testing** below.
   133	
   134	**Routing rule for development and testing (proposed).**
   135	
   136	| the question | use | then | evidence |
   137	|---|---|---|---|
   138	| Where is function or method X defined? | code graph | read that definition | BM-1 |
   139	| Where is a flag, constant or string defined? | full-text, or grep | read the hit | BM-1 (A05) |
   140	| What calls X? | code graph — lower bound, Python only | read the callers; treat the list as partial | BM-3 (C08–C10: 33–46 characters) |
   141	| What does changing F reach, several hops out? | code graph — Python only, lower bound | grep imports and callers for the rest, then read | G-F8, G-F17, G-F18 (not measured here) |
   142	| Which tests should I run after changing function X or file F? | code graph (tests reaching X or F) **plus** full-text on the file name (tests that run it as a separate process) | run that subset; run the full suite (31–33 s) before committing | BM-7, BM-8, BM-9 |
   143	| Why did we decide X? Where is X explained in the docs? | full-text (ties hybrid on hits; prints less in total) | read the section | BM-4 |
   144	| How do I run or configure X? | not settled: hybrid and vector scored 0.5 and full-text 0.33, on 6 questions | read the hit | BM-5 (n=6, too small to act on) |
   145	| A concept, in my own words | grep and read, as an agent search | record a miss if the answer is found another way | BM-2 |
   146	| Anything the graph answers with nothing or only partly | full-text, then grep | — | BM-8 |
   147	
   148	This table is for the **code and docs**. Part 1 above is for **memory entries**; it is reasoned, not measured, and
   149	three of its citations were corrected in this merge. The two
   150	differ because the corpus differs. Code and docs exist today and are already large enough that full-text and the
   151	graph save reading (BM-6). `project-memory/` does not exist yet.
   152	
   153	**What this does not change, and what it proposes.**
   154	- **Unchanged:** the rules for all three caches below (full rebuild, rebuild when stale, fall back when absent).
   155	  B1–Rd4 (Core requirements) are the acceptance criteria for the memory caches.
   156	- **The vision stands as confirmed:** for code and docs, the measurements say full-text and the graph already save
   157	  reading today. EJ kept the vision for memory entries only (2026-10-12), so the proposals in
   158	  `runs/20261012-caches-routing/vision-proposals.md` (P1–P3) were not adopted.
   159	
   160	## Rules for all three caches
   161	
   162	These apply to whichever cache is built. They come from how the researched tools fail.
   163	
   164	- **Full rebuild, never patched.** A live, incrementally updated index drifted silently from a clean rebuild: 4.3% of
   165	  edges wrong, with every integrity check passing (`G-F5 drift`). Its makers decided drift cannot be measured cheaply
   166	  (`G-F5c`). A full rebuild of this repo's code graph takes 0.94 s (`G-F12`); the benchmark's own code-graph rebuild
   167	  takes 0.89 s (BM-9); an FTS rebuild of 10,000 synthetic entries takes 0.53 s (`S-F13`).
   168	- **Rebuild before answering when stale.** Before each query, compare the recorded content hash of the
   169	  sources with the index and rebuild if they differ. Build into a temporary file and rename it over the old one
   170	  (`S-F9`, quoted: *"A stale index is worse than no index."*).
   171	- **Fall back when absent.** If the cache file or SQLite's FTS5 is missing, the tool answers with grep and says so in
   172	  every answer (Rd3.2) (`S-F9`); the same for a missing embedding model (my reading, extending `S-F9`). A mistyped
   173	  database path silently creates an empty database, which looks exactly like a stale one (`G-F5 drift`, failure
   174	  mode 5), so the tool opens the cache read-only and treats "no such file" as absent.
   175	- **Index only active entries** — for `project-memory/` — the same set as `INDEX.md`, with `status` and `covers`
   176	  stored beside each row (`V-F22`, my reading). Otherwise superseded entries come back looking current.
   177	
   178	## SQLite full-text index
   179	
   180	### Five whys
   181	
   182	1. **Why a full-text index?** To find entries by their words, ranked, with a snippet, instead of grep's unranked list
   183	   (`S-F1`, quoted: *"grep returns unranked matches."*).
   184	2. **Why does ranking matter?** Every hit the agent opens costs context. In the one controlled study, BM25 used 5.8K
   185	   tokens per question against 226K for an agent using grep over files, on its smallest corpus: 1,144 documents,
   186	   1.7M tokens (`S-F4`).
   187	3. **Why not build it now?** That study puts the point where BM25 overtakes the grep agent at around 10 million tokens
   188	   of text, and the grep agent led at the smallest sizes (`S-F4`). At our entry size that is about 37,000 entries
   189	   (`S-F5`, my reading via its 4-characters-per-token assumption). `INDEX.md` already lists every active entry. In the
   190	   researcher's own test, at 10,000 synthetic entries a one-word grep took 0.038 s and a grep chain for a header
   191	   question 0.06 s; at 50,000 the header chain took 0.15 s, and the one-word timing was invalid (`S-F13`). No study
   192	   covers a corpus of a few hundred to a few thousand short entries (`S-F5`: not measured).
   193	4. **Why might it be needed later?** `INDEX.md` stops being enough once it can no longer list every active entry under
   194	   its size limit. The agent then loses the one-line scan of every entry, and ranked search is what replaces it (my
   195	   reading).
   196	5. **Why only as a cache?** The markdown is the source of truth, and the cache must be rebuildable and skippable (M6).
   197	
   198	Structured questions such as "every active decision covering path X" or "what supersedes Y" do not need SQLite. A
   199	short script reading the entry headers answers them in 0.1 s at 10,000 entries (`S-F13`). That measures time, not
   200	tokens; my reading is that the script and an SQL query return the same list of entries, so the agent reads the same
   201	amount either way (not measured).
   202	
   203	### 5W1H
   204	
   205	| | |
   206	|---|---|
   207	| **Who** | Built and rebuilt by a script with no model call. Read by any agent on demand. EJ decides to build it when the trigger fires. |
   208	| **What** | One FTS5 table with `kind`, `status` and `covers` as `UNINDEXED` columns (`S-F2`). One row per active entry file: the research leaves the row grain open (`S-F17`); my reading is that an entry of one or two sentences is already one section. The default `unicode61` tokenizer: in the researcher's own one-row test, a plausible change to keep paths whole made ordinary queries return zero rows silently, and ids such as `D-0012` had to be quoted (`S-F16`). |
   209	| **When** | Built when the trigger fires: `INDEX.md` can no longer list every active entry. Rebuilt before any query that finds it stale. Never read at session start. |
   210	| **Where** | `project-memory/.cache/index.sqlite`, gitignored. |
   211	| **Why** | The five whys above. |
   212	| **How** | One stdlib `sqlite3` script, the smallest option in the SQLite node (§5, option B): rebuild to a temporary file and rename; compare the recorded content hash for staleness, so a deleted or uncommitted change is caught (K1.1, K2.1); fall back to `rg`. Nothing to install: SQLite 3.45.1 with FTS5 works here (verified 2026-10-12). |
   213

     1	# Source-of-Truth Memory Design
     2	
     3	> **Status: accepted by EJ on 2026-10-12** (EJ: *"yes I accept the source of truth design"*), with its vision and
     4	> its Write / Keep / Read requirements. Vision: `vision-statements/source-of-truth-memory-vision-statements.md`.
     5	> First written 2026-10-11 by `opencode` from
     6	> `runs/20261011-project-memory-engines/RECOMMENDATION.md`; revised with EJ on 2026-10-12.
     7	> Sources, citations and review history: `runs/20261011-source-of-truth-design/` (the full annotated version is
     8	> `DESIGN-detailed-2026-10-12.md`) and `runs/20261012-memory-update-timing/notes.md`.
     9	>
    10	> **Revised 2026-10-12:** the drift-check script and the background subagent are removed (EJ: *"ok remove both and
    11	> update the doc"*). A decision is started when it is written and complete when its time, result and test results are
    12	> added; whoever reads a decision or fact checks it against the code before acting on it.
    13	>
    14	> **Where this differs from the recommendation — EJ's decisions:** no owners and no human approval gate (anyone may
    15	> change any entry, and every change is logged); decisions and facts come before the code; a *fact* means a constraint
    16	> the code must respect, not something discovered afterwards; no drift-check script and no background subagent.
    17	>
    18	> **Not proven:** the timing split (what is written immediately and what later) is design judgement, not measured.
    19	> Where the recommendation's engines disagreed (session-start reads, one file per writer, SQLite, how often to write),
    20	> this design follows the majority.

    22	## Why we need this memory
    23	

codex
The main acceptance gaps are clear now: K3 says to include rebuild cost in a comparison measured in characters, without defining how, and Rd4’s check can pass when questions were omitted or recorded late. I also found code-graph build instructions still in the design despite the two-cache scope. I’m writing the review with concrete failure cases and proposed repairs, leaving policy choices for EJ.

codex
### Verdict

REPAIR — my reading: the two-cache design is supportable, but conflicting triggers, an undefined cost comparison, and incomplete failure checks prevent acceptance.

### Findings

All findings below are my reading. `CACHES.md` line numbers refer to the supplied snapshot.

1. **K3.1 — high — Rebuild cost has no comparable unit.**  
   **Evidence:** K3.1 says “a query’s cost includes any rebuild it triggered”; B1.1 measures “the characters read to the first right entry.” The K3 examples instead report rebuilds in seconds (`CACHES.md:568–579`). The previously accepted R8.2 compared rebuild time with reading time; the supplied diff removes that comparison.  
   **Breaking case:** a cache saves characters but takes minutes to rebuild. The text cannot determine whether it still pays. Its failure check becomes usable only after someone supplies an unstated cost rule.  
   **Proposed fix:** ask EJ which cost comparison governs retention, keeping unlike units separate unless he chooses a conversion. Define the measurement scope and the failure check without inventing a threshold.

2. **B1.1 — high — The full-text comparison lacks a complete scoring rule.**  
   **Evidence:** “after reading less … with no more misses” (`CACHES.md:366–370`) specifies characters but leaves the aggregation, result cutoff, and treatment of misses unresolved. The cited benchmark says its character mean is “over the answers that named one” and “A miss is left out” (`bench/results.md:28`).  
   **Breaking case:** full-text loses an expensive question that grep answers, gains a cheap question grep misses, and reports fewer characters with the same miss count. Different question sets can make that look like a saving.  
   **Proposed fix:** have EJ settle whether the comparison is per question or aggregate, which result cutoff defines a miss, and how unmatched successes are handled. Specify how the `INDEX.md` read contributes to the baseline cost.

3. **B1.1 / CACHES.md:70, 79, 209 — high — Two different full-text triggers remain operative.**  
   **Evidence:** B1.1 says full-text is built when its measured comparison wins. The routing table says “conditional on the size trigger,” and the 5W1H says “Built when … `INDEX.md` can no longer list every active entry.” The vision also names that size problem, but its success test uses B1.1’s comparison (`caches-vision.md:29–35, 47–49`).  
   **Breaking case:** the comparison passes while every active entry still fits in `INDEX.md`. B1.1 permits building; the 5W1H does not.  
   **Proposed fix:** ask EJ whether exceeding the index limit is a prerequisite or an example of when the comparison might win. Apply that answer consistently to the vision, routing table, five whys, and B1.1.

4. **B1.1 / CACHES.md:193–195 — high — The size-trigger explanation conflicts with the sibling design.**  
   **Evidence:** the caches text says that when the complete index no longer fits, “ranked search is what replaces it.” `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` K6.1 still requires “one line per active entry,” while Rd3.1 requires that file to fit the strictest start-file limit.  
   **Breaking case:** the complete active-entry list exceeds that limit. Installing full-text does not make the same complete `INDEX.md` fit, and Rd1 prohibits loading the cache at session start.  
   **Proposed fix:** leave the overflow behavior as an explicit owner decision and coordinate it with the sibling design. Do not imply that a search cache alone resolves the index’s incompatible obligations.

5. **Rd4 — medium — Its failure check does not enforce recording every question.**  
   **Evidence:** Rd4.1 requires “The questions agents ask … are recorded”; Rd4.2 requires recording “as it happens”; Rd4.3 specifies location, separate session files, and git. The check only fails when there are “no recorded questions” or a record lacks a field (`CACHES.md:478–491`).  
   **Breaking case:** an agent records one complete question after the session and omits every other question. The stated check passes despite violating Rd4.1 and Rd4.2.  
   **Proposed fix:** give completeness, timing, storage, and field validity their own checks. Treat blank or placeholder required values as invalid, while preserving the explicitly allowed “not found” answer.

6. **B1 / K3 — medium — The checks omit comparison and notification obligations.**  
   **Evidence:** B1 requires reruns when a miss is recorded and notification when a comparison passes; K3.2 requires reruns, and K3.3 requires telling EJ when the cache fails. Their checks inspect whether a cache exists without the necessary comparison or decision (`CACHES.md:372–393, 440–446`).  
   **Breaking case:** a recorded miss never triggers a rerun, or a passing trial is never reported to EJ. With no production cache installed, B1’s check does not fail.  
   **Proposed fix:** add checks for the specified rerun events and notifications. Clarify K3.1’s “keeps passing” against K3’s explicit allowance for EJ to retain a failed cache.

7. **B4.1 — medium — Agreement with `INDEX.md` is insufficient to establish active membership.**  
   **Evidence:** B4.1 requires “the same set of entries as `INDEX.md`.” V-F22’s inference instead depends on filtering the entry’s `status` field (`vector.claude.md:254–257`). The sibling design itself recognizes an out-of-step index as a failure in K6.  
   **Breaking case:** an entry becomes retired while `INDEX.md` still lists it as active. Copying that list either includes an inactive entry or conflicts with the “Only active entries” requirement.  
   **Proposed fix:** establish the active set from current entry headers and validate the generated index against it. Specify what a query does when those sets disagree.

8. **B3.1 — medium — “Fails if” does not check that rebuilding is complete.**  
   **Evidence:** B3.1 requires regeneration of “the whole index from the files,” but its check only asks whether “the rebuild has an update or incremental mode” (`CACHES.md:405–408`).  
   **Breaking case:** a command called `rebuild`, with no update option, silently reuses existing rows or vectors. The check passes without proving a full rebuild.  
   **Proposed fix:** check regeneration from the complete current source set as well as the absence of incremental paths, including paths outside the rebuild command.

9. **B2.1–B2.2 — medium — Verification preconditions are missing.**  
   **Evidence:** B2.1 promises identical entries after rebuilding with the same embedding model; B2.2 requires `git status --short` to be “clean.” K1.1 explicitly accommodates “edits not yet committed” (`CACHES.md:398–403, 424–426`).  
   **Breaking case:** rebuilding changes nothing tracked, but an existing uncommitted entry edit makes the check fail. Separately, unchanged model identity alone does not fix source contents or ranking configuration.  
   **Proposed fix:** hold the source snapshot and retrieval configuration constant for the repeatability check. Check that rebuilding introduces no tracked changes relative to the initial worktree state.

10. **K1.1 / K2.1 / B1.2 — medium — Some citations support an adaptation, not the stated mechanism.**  
    **Evidence:** K1.1 and K2.1 cite S-F9 for content-hash checking; S-F9 describes comparing build time with “the newest source mtime,” while S-F11 describes SHA-256. B1.2 cites V-F13 for its miss definition, but V-F13 explicitly says “the adaptation is INFERRED” (`vector.claude.md:151–168`).  
    **Breaking case:** a reader opens S-F9 expecting the cited hash mechanism and finds a different mechanism.  
    **Proposed fix:** retain the correct `(d)` and *proposed* labels, but distinguish the borrowed behavior from this design’s adaptation. Cite S-F11 for the hash precedent and mark the V-F13 miss definition as the researcher’s inference.

11. **CACHES.md:73–75, 160, 252–279 — medium — Code-graph instructions remain inside the operative design.**  
    **Evidence:** the core scope says “the code graph is its own project” (`CACHES.md:350–351`), yet Part 1 still routes memory questions to it, the rules cover “all three caches,” and its 5W1H still specifies `project-memory/.cache/code-graph.json`. The vision says there are “two optional indexes” (`caches-vision.md:21`).  
    **Breaking case:** an implementer follows the remaining 5W1H and installs a third cache in the memory project.  
    **Proposed fix:** replace operative graph instructions with a link to its project and update the title and cache count. Graph research may remain as clearly identified evidence for a shared failure class.

12. **B2.2 / B4.1 / K2.1 / Rd3.2 — low — Individual clauses combine independently failing obligations.**  
    **Evidence:** B2.2 combines git exclusion with rebuild cleanliness; B4.1 combines membership with metadata; K2.1 combines freshness comparison with atomic publication; Rd3.2 combines fallback routing with disclosure.  
    **Breaking case:** fallback works but its answer omits the disclosure. That is a different failure from fallback not working.  
    **Proposed fix:** split these clauses into separately identifiable obligations and checks. Preserve their existing sources and proposal status.

### Gaps

- **Stable source and index generations.** K2 needs an observable rule for source changes during hashing, rebuilding, or querying. Temporary-file rename prevents a partially written file from becoming visible; it does not prove that the published index represents one consistent source snapshot.
- **Consistent full-text and vector publication.** The vector 5W1H promises rebuilding both “in the same step, so the two cannot disagree,” but no requirement checks that a hybrid answer uses matching source generations. Failure between publishing the two stores needs defined behavior.
- **Active-entry filtering on fallback.** B4 constrains index membership, while Rd3 merely says to use grep. Ordinary grep over `entries/` can return superseded or retired entries, contrary to the sibling’s read restrictions; fallback needs the corresponding filtering behavior.
- **Safe interpretation of agent questions.** S-F16 reports that unquoted `D-0012` raises an FTS error. The design selects a tokenizer but gives no requirement for translating arbitrary question text into valid search input or handling malformed queries.
- **Replayable comparison evidence.** Rd4 records a question and an answering entry but does not establish the corpus revision, reproducible baseline searches, or how the expected answer is validated when entries later change. B1 and K3 need those facts to make their comparisons reviewable.

### What holds

- **B1.0:** the EJ decision gate is explicit and checkable; installing a cache after a passing comparison without his decision fails.
- **B1.2, retrieval condition:** a recorded missed entry recovered in the proposed top five provides a concrete success; losing a previously answered question provides a failure.
- **B2, derived-only intent:** deleting a cache must preserve memory; the vision and M4/M6 support that boundary.
- **B4, metadata condition:** a row missing `status` or `covers` concretely fails; V-F22 supports the labelled inference.
- **B5:** a full-text rebuild that calls a model fails; the updated vision directly supports the embedding exception.
- **K1.2:** missing embedding-model identity concretely fails; V-F24 supports invalidation on a model change.
- **K2, freshness outcome:** returning a deleted entry from the old index concretely fails.
- **Rd1:** a startup hook opening `project-memory/.cache/index.sqlite` concretely fails; the vision and sibling agree.
- **Rd2:** a result without its entry-file path concretely fails; M19 supports opening the primary source before acting.
- **Rd3:** a mistyped path that creates an empty database, or a failed rebuild that serves stale results, concretely fails; G-F5 and the labelled extensions support the behavior.
- **Rd4, provenance intent:** preserving question records outside the disposable cache and assigning their recording to the asking agent are coherent proposals.

### Not checked

- I checked the supplied design, vision, complete diff, sibling design, and relevant cited findings. I did not open other reviewers’ reviews.
- I did not verify original external publications, saved web copies, benchmark scripts, raw measurements, or the additional W/OC/SZ/CX sources behind the example tables.
- This is my manual requirements review; I ran no implementation tests. The benchmark questions were written by Codex and its harness by opencode, as the design discloses; those measurements do not establish performance on a real memory-entry corpus.
- Embedded EJ quotations were assessed as documentary attribution, without verification against the original conversation. No files were changed.
